Like a low hum. I keep thinking I should’ve seen one more thing coming, one more risk I missed. People tell me the controls were in place, but it doesn’t really settle anything. The guilt kind of follows me around.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Since the scaffold accident… during the business readjustment. So, a little over a year now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where your symptoms were significantly worse than they are now?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. It’s been pretty steady, I think. Some weeks are rougher than others, but I don’t know that I’d call any of it a big change.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things that normally bring people pleasure, like eating or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Some of it, I guess, but not like before. My wife and kids will be laughing at dinner, or I’ll get through a tough project milestone, and I know that should feel good. Mostly I just feel kind of flat… like I’m watching it from a step back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s kind of what pushed me to come in, honestly. Sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, messing around in the woodshop… they used to help. Now they just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely, but my clothes seem about the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt really down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like a big crash, exactly. More like this low-level dread that’s just there most days. And guilt, I guess. Like I should’ve caught one more thing, seen one more risk coming… even though people keep telling me the safeguards were in place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to feel pleasure in things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… I think that’s the main thing. The sailing races, the historical society shift on Saturdays, even working in the woodshop — they used to help keep me steady. Now I just keep putting them off, like they’re chores.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t really seek people out much lately. Even the sailing races or my Saturday shift at the historical society—things that used to keep me balanced—now just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

My wife and kids will be laughing at dinner, or I’ll get through a tough project milestone, and I know that should feel good. Mostly I just feel kind of flat… like I’m watching it from a step back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely, but my clothes seem about the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt really down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like a big crash, exactly. More like this low-level dread that’s just there most days. And guilt, I guess. Like I should’ve caught one more thing, seen one more risk coming… even though people keep telling me the safeguards were in place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to feel pleasure in things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… I think that’s the main thing. The sailing races, the historical society shift on Saturdays, even working in the woodshop — they used to help keep me steady. Now I just keep putting them off, like they’re chores.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t really seek people out much lately. I mean… I think that’s the main thing. The sailing races, the historical society shift on Saturdays, even working in the woodshop — they used to help keep me steady. Now I just keep putting them off, like they’re chores.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t really seek people out much lately. Even the sailing races or my Saturday shift at the historical society—things that used to keep me balanced—now just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low-level dread and lack of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Since the scaffold accident… during the business readjustment. So, a little over a year now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I can still do things, but I don’t get much out of them. Sailing, the historical society, messing around in the woodshop… they all just feel like things I’m supposed to do, so I put them off.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I mean… those were the things I used to look forward to—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, messing around in the woodshop. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt really down or depressed for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. It’s not like I’m in bed crying or anything, but there’s been this steady dread and guilt that just kind of follows me around. My wife and kids will be laughing at dinner, or I’ll get through a tough project milestone, and I know that should feel good. Mostly I just feel kind of flat… like I’m watching it from a step back.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. That’s kind of what pushed me to come in, honestly. Sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, messing around in the woodshop… they used to help. Now they just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I haven’t been tracking it closely, but my clothes seem about the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last year where you felt really down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… not like a big crash, exactly. More like this low-level dread that’s just there most days. And guilt, I guess. Like I should’ve caught one more thing, seen one more risk coming… even though people keep telling me the safeguards were in place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to feel pleasure in things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I mean… I think that’s the main thing. The sailing races, the historical society shift on Saturdays, even working in the woodshop — they used to help keep me steady. Now I just keep putting them off, like they’re chores.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in social activities or being around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess so. I don’t really seek people out much lately. Even the sailing races or my Saturday shift at the historical society—things that used to keep me balanced—now just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this low-level dread and lack of interest in activities?\n\nPatient: Since the scaffold accident… during the business readjustment. So, a little over a year now, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean… I can still do things, but I don’t get much out of them. Sailing, the historical society, messing around in the woodshop… they all just feel like things I’m supposed to do, so I put them off.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still look forward to or enjoy?\n\nPatient: Not much, no. I mean… those were the things I used to look forward to—sailing races, my Saturday shift at the historical society, messing around in the woodshop. Lately they just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt really down or depressed for two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I suppose so. It’s not like I’m in bed crying or anything, but there’s been this steady dread and guilt that just kind of follows me around. Like I should’ve seen one more risk coming, even though people keep telling me the controls were in place.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost the ability to feel pleasure in things that you used to find enjoyable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. That’s kind of what made me agree to come in. Sailing races, the historical society on Saturdays, even working in the woodshop… I used to care about those. Now they just feel like chores I keep putting off.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of dread and guilt usually present for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I’d say it’s there a lot. Not always sharp, just this low-grade dread and guilt hanging around, like I missed something I should’ve caught.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling this way for more than two years?\n\nPatient: No… not two years. It started after the scaffold accident, during the business readjustment. So… a little over a year now.\n\nDoctor: Have your feelings of dread and guilt been present for most of the day, every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah. For at least a couple weeks, probably more. It’s… most of the day, there’s this low-grade dread and guilt, like I should’ve seen one more risk coming, even though people keep saying the controls were there.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
